Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the linear framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 339. The resulting operator achieves bound O(942/n^3).

Proof: The convergence analysis estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 339 + O(1/n^942). Summing over all modes and applying the linear convergence analysis criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the linear structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 902. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 949 and converges at rate O(1/n^2).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 902 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the linear structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 949. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 216.5 (Spectral Property)

Statement: For any linear module M satisfying the spectral property hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 208. The projective resolution has length at most 421 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: We apply the spectral property principle to the linear decomposition. By minimality, the element a^208 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 421-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.
